The check therefore routes the tunnel's bytes into an internal listener (`envoy.bootstrap.internal_listener`), a listener inside the same Envoy process that receives a connection from another Envoy filter rather than from the network. The CONNECT route passes the expected host to it through shared filter state. The internal listener runs `tls_inspector`, then an RBAC filter whose CEL condition compares the server name with the expected host, and then forwards with `sni_dynamic_forward_proxy` and `tcp_proxy`.
  - **Round 3 result.** The check works at about 175 lines of YAML. A matching server name passes. A different name, a missing name, or an IP-literal authority is refused. The cost is about 0.2 ms at p50 and 15 to 25% more Envoy CPU per tunnel.
  - **Caveats.** The decision service lower-cases the authority, because Envoy does not. Non-TLS payloads close or pass by policy, and server-first protocols wait for `listener_filters_timeout`, which defaults to 15 s. Missing headers render as `-` in `set_filter_state` until `omit_empty_values: true` is set. On this route an SSRF refusal arrives as 200 followed by EOF rather than 503. Encrypted Client Hello defeats the check when the client sets the outer public name to the authority: the server received a different inner name.
  - **Status.** Revision 2 listed the check as later work because it had not been built. Revision 3 places it in proposal 2 as a per-host-set setting, on by default for TLS host sets, on for a `*` ceiling, and off for hosts that carry non-TLS protocols. Squid can make the same check by peeking without decrypting.
- **DNS channel.**
  - Today one dedicated agent CoreDNS forwards every name upstream. An agent can encode data in queries for a domain it controls, and the attacker's DNS server receives them. The rate limit (10 responses per second per source) and the TXT size filter slow this, but A and AAAA queries still carry data.
  - Pods in `proxied` pools have no need for external names, because clients send the hostname inside CONNECT. The second spike confirmed this for Go, Node's undici, .NET, and CPython. Java's `URL.equals` resolves names, but it falls back to comparing strings when resolution fails.
  - The fix is a second CoreDNS Deployment for `proxied` pods. It answers cluster names and returns NXDOMAIN immediately for everything else. Dropping queries would stall Java.
  - The controller gives `proxied` pods that instance's address as their nameserver. That needs a per-profile DNS address, since today one address applies to every pod. The `proxied` NetworkPolicy allows only that instance.
  - The cost is one small Deployment plus a controller change for the per-profile address. Revision 2 listed it as later work because the existing `restricted` pods carry the same DNS channel today, so the gap is not new. The channel matters more once `proxied` pods run agents with open web access, and round 3 found no client that needs external names behind the proxy, so revision 3 moves the resolver into proposal 2.
  - Round 3 ran it on kind. Cluster names resolved in 0.3 to 4 ms, external names returned NXDOMAIN in about 1 ms after walking the search list, and curl, Python requests and pip, Node 24 fetch, and Go reached public HTTPS sites through the proxy with no local lookup of the target name. Node without `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY` failed fast with ENOTFOUND rather than stalling.
- **Level 1 replacement.** See "Level 1 replacement", **What level 1 gives up**.
- **Platform trust boundary.** `lenny-ops` and the controller can reach the egress PKI through their existing rights (see "Transport security").
- **Stored-token push.** See "Credentials".
- **Uncovered git hosts.** On a host no VCS pool, git route, or binding of the tenant names, git goes through an opaque tunnel with no git enforcement. `netagent` sees the CONNECT authority alone, so there is no path check, no refusal of a push, no `allowedRefPatterns`, no push option refusal, no `git.operation` record, no mirror route, no `sshForms` mapping, and no fetch size cap. A pinned-SHA `gitClone` also skips the resolver's host check. A token the agent holds reaches the host **[measured]**, a run's push ended non-fast-forward, so a push completing there is inferred from another candidate, and a push to a server an attacker runs needs no token. This is a loss against the prior path on its rewrite path, and the owner took it with decision 44. A hybrid that keeps the prior path for these hosts was not measured, and the design has none. What the design gives instead:
  - The effective policy still decides which hosts a CONNECT can reach, and `egress.tunnel` records each tunnel with bytes in each direction, except at level 1, where `egress.local` carries it.
  - A tenant that needs enforcement on a host adds a git route to `direct` or to the mirror, which makes it covered. A fetch of a public repository then passes and a push is refused unless a pool covers the host. It costs a CA entry.
  - Under EG-12, `credentialPinned` on the host refuses it with no binding.
  - The minted token and a binding's secret never go there, so what reaches the host is a token the agent already held.
  - **The alias limit.** Coverage names host names. An allowed sibling name for the same service, or on a `*` ceiling an agent's own DNS name at the host's public address, is opaque, and an agent's own token works there if the host accepts it. Shipped host data adds a provider's alias hosts, and the server-name check, which is on for a `*` ceiling, refuses a mismatched name and an IP literal. How GitHub and GitLab edges treat an unknown server name is not measured. On an open ceiling the agent can already send data to its own host.
- **Tree validation.** Pod-side git skips the gateway's tree validation, which happens at materialization:
  - Today the gateway clones a `gitClone` source itself, packs the tree into a tar, decodes it inside the gateway through the archive gate (§7.1 step 12, §13.4, `gatedExtract`), and streams the files into the pod. The gate rejects absolute paths, `..`, excessive depth and length, hardlinks, device files, FIFOs, and sockets. It confines symlink targets to the slot and away from `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, and `/run/lenny`. It strips setuid, setgid, and sticky bits, and it bounds total size, entry size, entry count, and compression ratio.
  - When the pod fetches with git, most of these threats cannot arise, because git trees hold only regular files with mode 644 or 755, directories, symlinks, and submodule links. git's checkout always rejects `.git` entries in any spelling, `..`, and writes through symlinks. With `transfer.fsckObjects=true`, fetch also rejects malformed trees and unsafe `.gitmodules`.
  - Two checks remain uncovered. git checks out symlinks that point outside the tree as written. And nothing bounds a repository's size before checkout.
  - The design closes both. A component that runs as the adapter or `netagent` UID never follows workspace symlinks (checkpoint and export already re-validate them). `netagent` and the mirror cap fetch size per pool. §4.7 **Hard workspace size limit.** also requires `emptyDir.sizeLimit` on the slot volume, but `podspec/volumes.go` sets none today, so the kubelet does not bound a fetch the agent runs. That gap is filed as a finding and closed in proposal 2, because the design relies on it.
  - The §13.4 exception states this mapping. When the agent UID runs git itself, the result is equivalent to files the agent could create at runtime.

## Specification sections affected

- §2 Goals and Non-Goals: "gateway-mediated file delivery", "Git-based or object-store-based workspace population by pods", and "no standing credentials in pods", which the gated opt-ins qualify.
- §4.7.1 and §28.5.1: the materialization RPC carries a `gitClone` source with its resolved SHA to the pod instead of streamed files.
- §4.7: the `netagent` container, the local egress endpoint, `/run/netagent` and the network descriptor, the launch variables in the supervisor environment, and the removal of `git-credential-lenny`.
- §4.9: provider capability levels, minting, token caching, repository routing, and credential bindings with their delivery modes.
- §10.2: the `tenant_id` pattern narrows to a valid Kubernetes label value, at most 63 characters with alphanumeric ends, because level 1 policies select on `lenny.dev/tenant-id`, the label stamp already fails on a longer identifier, and no deployment depends on the wider form.
- §5.1 and §5.2: the pool egress ceiling, the warm-up policy, `gitAccess`, the client proxy profile, pool ownership, policy granularity per pod model, and the reservation of the launch variable names in the Runtime `env` rule.
- §7.1: steps 11 to 13 (clone in the pod) and 0111's **Bind bounds.**.
- §7.3: the provisional binding during a resume rebuild, and the 30 s fetch wait on that path.
- §6.1 and §6.4: warm-up egress and the warm fill.
- §6.2: the `git_fetch_pending` materialization failure reason and the fetch retry window against the finalize bound.
- §7.5: setup-phase network access, which replaces "network blocked by default during setup" for `proxied` pools.
- §10.2: platform-admin-only `egressProfile` writes on shared pools, and the tenant pool overlay in the "own tenant" scoping note.
- §10.3 and §13.2: the egress PKI, separate from the `mtls.enabled` PKI, and TLS on I2, I3, I5, and I7, in line with the cross-namespace posture §13 already states for the OTLP and object-store links.
- §10.3: the two `netagent` token audiences beside the gateway audience, and the Token Service as a per-tenant OIDC issuer.
- §12.8: purge of tenant stores in the mirror under `DeleteByTenant`, and the retirement of a tenant's service-mode pods before their pin records.
- §12.9: classification of mirror contents, federation tokens, credential bindings, and pin records.
- §25.11: rebuilding pin records from pod labels after a restore.
- §11.7, §12, §12.8, §12.9, §15, and §16: the evidence streams, `EvidenceStore`, their anchor in the audit chain, erasure, classification, retention, and read APIs.
- §13.2 and §28: the service-mode listener's ingress and gateway egress rows and its register entry.
- §13.1: the egress, file delivery, and credentials rows ("No standing credentials" gains the gated exceptions), the `netagent` container identity, the webhook coverage of `netagent`'s volumes, and the mirror container's capability exception.
- §13.2.2: the base policy, which `proxied` and `gitAccess` extend.
- §13.2.5: the exclusion list gains the ranges listed under "Central egress proxy", and the SSRF check on tenant-registered services.
- §13.2.7: the second resolver for `proxied` pods beside the dedicated agent CoreDNS.
- §13.3: credential bindings and federation tokens in the credential flow.
- §13.2.4: `lenny-system` rows for `lenny-egress`, `lenny-egress-authz`, `lenny-evidence`, and `lenny-git-mirror`, including the Token Service's path to `lenny-egress` for mints and every producer's path to `lenny-evidence`; the Token Service's ingress row gains the agent namespaces on the I8 port, and its egress gains the decision service, the pool and session stores, the API server pod watch over the agent namespaces and `lenny-system`, and the provider APIs.
- §4.9 and §10.3: the Token Service's new reads (binding definitions and session bindings through `pkg/binding`) and its I8 listener beside the gateway-only mTLS listener.
- §13.2.6: the `proxied` profile, the removal of `provider-direct` (NET-006 and NET-044), the DNS resolver for `proxied` pods, and the IPv4-mapped and NAT64 exclusions.
- §13.4: the tree-validation exception for pod-side git.
- §14: the `gitClone` row (pod-side clone, ref resolution through `ls-refs` in Go, and no gateway clone), `writableRepositories`, `egress.allowedHosts`, session credential bindings, the `origin` remote, the reserved `env` names, and the removal of the in-pod credential helper.
- §15.1: the `GIT_CLONE_REF_*` rows keep their meaning with `ls-refs`, and the new egress, binding, and clone error codes.
- §15.7: the network accessor in the runtime SDKs.
- §16: the metrics and alerts of the new components.
- §17.2 and §17.8: the namespace layout, the admission inventory (the third UID), the mirror container's capability exception, its Pod Security prerequisite, its anti-affinity and `hostUsers` value, placement, and sizing.
- §18: the materializer entry for `gitClone`.
- §21.9 and §26.3: the in-pod token-helper references.
- §24 and §25: `lenny-ctl` commands and operability tools, as the documentation section lists.
- §15: admin API resources for host sets, upstreams, git endpoints, tenant egress policy, tenant pool overlays, credential bindings, per-class gate grants, client proxy profiles, tenant client config files, and mirror prefetch.
- §13.1, §13.2, §15, §16, and §17: level 1 replacement, which covers the policy document and EDL endpoints, identities and their rotation, the I7 decision call, the validators' refusals, the probe, the alert, and the Helm values.
- §15.1: the `egress_denied` and `no_egress_path` reasons on `GIT_CLONE_REF_UNRESOLVABLE`.
- §23: "pods can only reach the gateway".
- §26.2: the removal of category-keyed defaults (egress, isolation, delivery mode, setup command policy, and `preConnect`), descriptive `defaultPoolConfig` text per reference runtime, and the `vcs.<provider>.*` scopes.
- §28: the new links and channels. The labels I1 to I8 are design-local; the proposal assigns `LNK-` and `CH-` identifiers under the naming law, which forbids positional names. §28.5.5's register of the pod-egress boundary (`CH-LLMPROXY` and `CH-OBJSTORE`) gains the new channels, and `CH-LLMPROXY` itself is unchanged.

## Relationship to proposal 0111 and open findings

Proposal 0111 is rewritten around this design.

- **Kept unchanged**: the slot repository rules (alternates, capture copy, and `storeCommit`), store immutability, the §7.1 **Bind bounds.**, and the defect filings. 0111's `ValidateGitURL` is replaced by the shared canonicalizer. 0111's `Dockerfile.git` for the gateway is dropped, because the gateway runs no git; F-14.1.37 closes with the clone path's removal.
- **Kept with changes**:
  - The warm fill and `gitRepository` shared asset are fetched by `netagent` over smart HTTP.
  - Private stores keyed by owner tenant are authorized through I3.
  - The mirror semantics move into `lenny-git-mirror`.
  - Restore and lineage authorization are kept. With `FetchGitPack` gone, nothing server-side binds a restore fetch to a repository, and `lenny.gitStoreUrl` is agent-writable, so restore completion fetches only a URL the gateway passes for that bind: the plan's repositories and any the lineage rule authorizes. The gateway passes that set on `FinalizeWorkspace` and on Resume, which replaces 0111's statement that Resume gains no field.
  - The §13.4 exception is rewritten as above.
  - The `git-https` provider becomes a `stored` provider.
  - The pod identity prerequisite is met by the projected service account token instead of R14.
- **Dropped**: the `FetchGitPack` RPC, gateway pack serving, the gateway mirror limiters and memory table, and the gateway memory limit decision.
- **For a tenant's default clone**: `tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md`, "Relationship to draft proposal 0111", builds on this rewrite and replaces the declaration, the carrier, the refusal of a full commit, the unsized store, and the absence of refresh and backoff. An unbound owned pod's readable set is its warm list and the base's public `gitRepository` entries alone, and the decision service and the Token Service read that list from the owned pool row. A tenant's store serves only a default clone. On a pod with no store, a new session takes a shallow fetch by SHA from the adapter, and restore completion fetches shallow first and fetches history only when its listing finds a missing object, as for a repository whose pinned commit was amended or reset, within the slot's remaining bytes.

Findings closed or reshaped by this work:

- F-4.9.44 (minting).
- F-14.1.37 (git binary).
- The §26.2 and §13.2.6 contradiction (F-26.2.12).
- The unenforced mode and missing plan check in `lenny/vcs_token` (F-26.2.11).
- The Token Service breaker exposure from in-pod token requests.
- The tenant-admin write of `egressProfile` on shared pools (F-5.2.67).

These defects exist in the code today, independent of this design, and are filed in `BUILD-GAPS.md`:

| Finding | Severity | Defect | Closed by |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| F-5.2.65 | High | Service-mode pins have no arbiter across gateway replicas, and a label failure does not stop the request (`tenantaffinity.go` and `statelessproxy.go`), so a service-mode pool with more than one gateway replica can route one tenant's request to a pod pinned to another. §5.2's in-memory mapping cannot arbitrate either. | Proposal 0 |
| F-5.2.66 | High | The service-mode request path is not wired: `pkg/adapter/statelessslot` has no production caller, and the chart's policies do not admit the gateway's dial to the runtime port. | Proposal 0 |
| F-25.6.9 | High | `lenny-ctl doctor --fix` (`doctorremediator.go`) matches every 24-hour leaf against a 7-day window and deletes its Secret, with no `isCA` filter, so it can rotate a CA's key out of order, and the health API reports every healthy install as `unhealthy`. §25.6 specifies this, so the specification changes too. | SO-6 in `security-oversight-design.md` |
| F-25.4.40 | Medium | `lenny-ops` holds `get`, `list`, `watch`, and `delete` on every Secret in the release namespace (`ops-rbac.yaml`), which includes the `lenny-mtls-ca` key, alongside the Job and Certificate rights that reach it another way. | SO-7 in `security-oversight-design.md` |
| F-5.2.67 | High | A `tenant-admin` can change `egressProfile` and every other field of a shared pool (`applyPoolUpdate` has no role check), so one tenant sets the network posture of pods that serve other tenants. | Proposal 9 |
| F-26.2.11 | High | `lenny/vcs_token` returns the pool's stored token to any session in the tenant, with no check of the plan, the repository, or the mode. | Proposal 3, with minting in proposal 1 |
| F-26.2.12 | Medium | §26.2 describes an `egressProfile` allowlist and an `unrestricted` value that §13.2.6 does not define. | Proposal 9 |
| F-13.2.28 | Medium | The label webhooks do not cover `pods/status`, so a holder of that subresource can change every immutable label and `lenny.dev/tenant-id` with no webhook consulted. | Proposal 6 |
| F-13.2.29 | Low | `DecideImmutableLabels` treats an unset label and an empty value as equal, so an `UPDATE` can add or remove an immutable label with an empty value. The new decider under egress groups checks map presence and does not change the immutable list. | A direct fix |
| F-13.2.31 | Medium | The NET-003 creation guard is not enforced: any principal that can create a pod in an agent namespace can set `lenny.dev/managed: "true"` and the egress labels. The chart grants that right to `lenny-controller` alone, and an agent pod has no path. | A direct fix, with §13.2's identity through the proposal pipeline |

## Reserved for other designs

These hooks are in this design's proposals because adding them later would be rework. Each names the document that owns the feature. None of them does anything until that feature lands, except where stated.

| Hook | Proposal | Why now | Owner |
| :-- | :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `owner_tenant_id`, `template_pool`, and `pod_runtime_ref` on the pool row | EG-9, and DR-2 for the last | Stored format | `tenant-derived-runtimes-design.md` |
| A `quarantined` binding state in `pkg/binding`, set by a `lenny.dev/quarantine` label or an active `pod_quarantines` row and taking precedence over every other state: the decision service refuses all egress, the Token Service mints nothing, and the mirror serves nothing. Level 1's tenant policies carry the same selector exclusion. | EG-2, EG-7 | It touches the enforcement point in three components, and it denies traffic as soon as a state is set | `forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md` |
| A held flag on mirror stores in the store index, which the sweep's re-chown, repacks, and purge honour. The index persists the fetch UIDs that own files in a held store and keeps them out of the pool across a restart, and the on-disk path includes a store generation, so a held generation and its successor coexist. | EG-5 | Those paths would otherwise need rework once shipped | `forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md` |
| One write path for every opt-in (bindings, gates, VCS pool write settings, tenant-registered endpoints, routes, always-allowed and session-requestable hosts, warm-up additions, client config files, overlays, derived runtimes and the derivation grant, and the shadow policy), which applies a tenant policy check, empty today, and emits an audit event | EG-9 | Guardrails need a single choke point | `security-oversight-design.md` |
| A nullable `suspendedBy` on every opt-in object, which the effective policy, the Token Service, `netagent`, and the decision service treat as absent, except that a running session's qualification under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials` still counts a suspended binding, with the effective-policy read naming the guardrail layer, a withdrawn gate, a pin, or a VCS pool that suspended it | EG-9, EG-2, EG-4 | A tightening must reach existing objects, and the check would otherwise be added to several components | `security-oversight-design.md` |
| Writable set entries as records with a `state` (`granted`, the only value EG-3 sets, and later `pendingApproval`) and an optional `expiresAt`, with any other state read only to both gates and `netagent`, except that `userRefused` also denies reads of that repository. An unknown state on a readable entry denies access. | EG-2, EG-3 | The writable set is in the binding record and feed | `security-oversight-design.md` |
| `pkg/binding` returns the session's environment and `crossedFromEnvironment`, and the decision service, the Token Service, and the mirror reach tenant objects through one resolver taking tenant and environment, which filters nothing until TS-1 | EG-2 | The enforcement point exists in several components | `tenant-scoping-identity-design.md` |
| Named permissions on every new read (`evidence.read.<stream>`, `egress.effectivePolicy.read`), which `tenant-admin` holds by default | EG-8 and EG-9 | A new role is then a new grant, with no handler changes | `security-oversight-design.md` |
| The evidence envelope, its `provenance` values and `payloadKeyRef`, per-stream retention and permissions, and a held marker on manifests | EG-10 | New streams, such as agent activity, are then additive, and a hold needs no format change | `forensics-evidence-preservation-design.md` |
| Typed route targets and a per-kind registration validator | EG-2 | A connector then needs no new route or contract | `tenant-outbound-connector-design.md` |
| A write refuses two VCS pools on one host whose flavours differ. The flavour derives from provider and host, as `user-credential-delivery-design.md` states, with no stored field. | EG-1 | The pool write is a shipped validator. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| An expected route and CA on I8 responses for a `userCredential` binding, with the route asserted on the CONNECT by `netagent` and by the Token Service on a link provider call, in the level 2 contract, and with the decision service refusing a disagreement, its cache key holding the asserted list, and the level 1 I7 decision call carrying it | EG-2, EG-4, EG-7 | A wire contract on I2 and I8, and a shipped decision. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| A `handling` value and a mint refusal for a session qualifying under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`, limited to `netagent`'s requests, so an image without the attach rule refuses the release rather than minting | EG-3, EG-4 | The git path drops a client's credential in the first shipped image. UC-5 owns the field and the rule. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| `cacheUntil` on I8 responses, honoured by `netagent` | EG-2, EG-4 | A wire contract and a cache rule. An image that ignores it holds a token past revocation with no error. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| `handling` on I8 responses, with `netagent` refusing what it does not implement | EG-4 | The refusal has to be in the first shipped image, or an older one attaches a credential without a required step. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| The request inspection stage in `inject`, able to refuse and to replace the forwarded path, headers, body, and query string, evaluating `requestRules` from I8 | EG-4 | `inject` is a shipped enforcement point on both transports, and canonical forwarding is what closes a parser difference a capture found. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| One `inject` binding per host and session, one `userCredential` binding per type host, and no `file` beside an `inject` binding, on type hosts, and `scope: poolAttach` | EG-4 | Which binding answers on a shared host is otherwise unspecified, and fixing it later changes a shipped decision. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| `secret` as a typed union, with `userCredential` and unknown kinds absent | EG-4 | Stored record format. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| The `userCredentialDelivery` gate, empty until UC-5 | EG-9 | One write path and one gate model. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| A pre-mint step before the mint cache lookup, empty until UG-2 | EG-1, EG-2 | The order of a check and the cache is a shipped enforcement point. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| Readable entries as records with `verifiedAt`, and a declared-only input on the readable formula | EG-2, EG-3, EG-9 | The binding record and feed are stored and on the wire. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| The repository named in the mirror's second-gate I3 check | EG-5, EG-2 | The mirror is the path with no mint. TS-1's mirror filter needs the same field. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| `pkg/binding` returns the session's user, root session, caller type, and the user's invalidation level. `caller_type` becomes a field on the session row, because only the request principal carries it today, and a delegated child copies its parent's value. | EG-2 | The same reason as the environment hook. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| A `userCredential` binding excluded from the provisional binding until `running` | EG-2 | The §7.3 edit is staged there. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| A delegated child receives a `userCredential` binding only when the lease names it | EG-9 | The rule for children is under "Effective policy". | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| A `required` VCS pool as a source of `credentialPinned`, read across the tenant unfiltered by environment | EG-12 | The pin's sources are read by a shipped enforcement point. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| A link provider's endpoints on the mint path, through `lenny-egress` whatever the route | EG-2 | The decision service's tenant assertion is a shipped check. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| Push option refusal, with bounded parsing past the ref commands | EG-3 | The push path is a shipped enforcement point. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| A head read deadline, and refusal of a bare LF, on `netagent`'s listeners | EG-2, EG-4 | A defect of the listener with or without rules. | `user-git-identity-design.md` |
| `lockEgressToHosts` in the session formulas | EG-9 | A formula every enforcement point reads. | `user-credential-delivery-design.md` |
| `lenny.dev/doctor-optout` on the egress CA and leaf Certificates | EG-11 | Today's `doctor --fix` remedy deletes a matched Certificate's Secret, and the annotation is a hard skip. It protects these certificates until the remedy is fixed, so this design does not wait for that fix. | `security-oversight-design.md` (SO-6) |

Region and tenant key fields on mirror stores and evidence objects are in scope here rather than hooks. The mirror honours a tenant's `dataResidencyRegion`: a tenant whose region the mirror cannot serve routes `direct` for its private repositories, as a non-`mint-scoped` provider does.


## Proposal split

0. **Service-mode tenant pin** (F-5.2.65 and F-5.2.66): the durable pin record, label authority, UID-keyed routing and labeling, the snapshot age limit, the service-mode listener with its dial check and network rows, the lifecycle and restore rules, and the §5.2 change. It closes a defect that exists today, it depends on no other proposal, and it should run first. `pkg/binding`'s reading of the record lands with proposal 2.
1. **Git prerequisites**: GitHub App minting with provider capability levels and the token cache (F-4.9.44), repository identifiers, the shared repository-path canonicalizer, repository routing, and write-scope checks. The `github` provider keeps accepting a stored `token` until proposal 3 removes its consumers, so proposal 1 lands without breaking `gitClone`.
2. **Pod network agent and proxied egress**: the `tenant_id` pattern, the removal of `provider-direct`, server TLS on I2 and I7 and mTLS on I3, I3 operations by client SAN, with the chart's reload, readiness, mount, CPU, and alert rules, the producers of `egress.decision`, `egress.tunnel`, `egress.local`, and `credential.mint`, the `quarantined` binding state, typed route targets and the per-kind validator, the Token Service's mint path through `lenny-egress`, the binding push and cache rule for pins, `netagent`, service account identity, `lenny-egress` on Envoy, `lenny-egress-authz` with I3 and I7, the binding feed with fan-out and the read-scoped provisional resume binding (§7.3), the owner label on tenant-owned pods at creation (§5.2), port scoping, the Kubernetes issuer verifier, the I8 listener, the `pkg/binding` resolution package, and the Token Service's new RBAC and ingress rows, the CA bundle assembly, the tunnel lifetime menu, quotas, the `proxied` profile, and the DNS resolver for `proxied` pods.
3. **Pod git path** (F-26.2.11): GitLab token reuse, the `git.operation` stream from `netagent`, `allowedRefPatterns`, commit identity, `gitAccess` and its effective-policy formula, the covered host set with its push and its alias data, the per-pod CA and its name constraints, termination and the git path on the egress endpoint with its fail-closed rules and LFS response handling, the terminated marker for covered hosts in decisions and in the decision cache's key, the generation wait before materialization, the bundle in git's trust, push with minted tokens, `writableRepositories`, the rendered git config and per-host `sshForms` rules, the `origin` remote, LFS, redirect handling, git in the adapter image, the pod-side clone, the gateway's Go `ls-refs` resolver through `lenny-egress`, the removal of the gateway clone path (which closes F-14.1.37), the workspace ownership fix, and the removal of `git-credential-lenny` and `lenny/vcs_token`.
4. **Credential delivery**: credential bindings with `inject` delivery (plain-HTTP upgrade, and EG-3's termination extended to bound hosts), `federation` delivery with the Token Service as OIDC issuer and the AWS, GCP, and Azure templates, `metadata` delivery with the AWS plugin, and `file` delivery with the generic directory and the `netrc` projection. It also covers the network descriptor, the SDK network accessor, and tenant client config files.
5. **Git mirror and warm fill**: the held flag and region and key fields on stores, the `git.operation` stream from the mirror, `lenny-git-mirror` serving TLS, with its anti-affinity, `gitMirror.hostUsers` and its start check, the ID ranges and fetch UID reuse rule, its sandbox and capability exception, per-store ownership, the fetch retry window, the mirror prefetch resource, and the rewritten 0111.
6. **Per-tenant routing and replacement** (F-13.2.28): upstream registrations and chaining (I6), private destination pinning, egress groups with API assignment, exclusivity, and the `lenny.dev/egress-group` label, per-endpoint and per-binding CAs, tenant-registered public services, and deployer CA trust distribution.
7. **Level 1 replacement**: identities and their rotation, the policy document, its host grammar, the EDL endpoint, the reference Squid renderer, the I7 decision call and tunnel reports, the `netagent` server-name check, the service identities for the resolver and the mirror, the Helm-rendered private destination grants and tenant-scoped policies, the direct path for `netagent`, the resolver, and the mirror, the validators' refusals, the acknowledgement, the install order with its preflight and post-install probe, the canary, and the documentation of its limits.
8. **Tenant visibility**: decision queries and export, the effective-policy read, the shadow policy, the egress address read, and fixed-name files for more registries.
9. **Egress policy objects** (F-5.2.67 and F-26.2.12): pool ownership (`ownerTenantId`, taken from draft 0111 because the formulas depend on it), ceilings, warm-up policies, tenant egress policy, session `egress.allowedHosts`, tenant pool overlays, host sets, the client proxy profile, the removal of category-keyed defaults from §26.2, the `egressProfile` write restriction, the per-class gate grants, one write path for opt-ins, and named permissions on the new reads.
10. **Evidence ingest**: the envelope, `lenny-evidence`, `EvidenceStore`, the compactor, the anchor, retention, erasure, the held marker, and the regulated write-ahead path.
11. **Egress PKI**: the egress CA, its Issuer and leaves with their `lenny.dev/doctor-optout` annotation, the `lenny-egress-ca` ConfigMap, and the rotation procedure, with no consumer until the others use it.
12. **Exfiltration controls**: `credentialPinned` and read-only hosts, added to EG-3's terminated marker, and name constraints that cover those hosts. The other proposals do not wait for it, and until it lands the opaque-tunnel limits under "Residual risks and limits" apply to every host EG-3 does not cover.

Proposals 0, 1, 9, and 11 are independent. Proposal 10 depends on proposal 11 for its certificates and lands with no producer until the others use it, so a decision service never ships without its evidence format. Proposal 2 depends on proposals 0, 1, 9, 10, and 11, since its Token Service minting path needs proposal 1, and it lands I8, `pkg/binding`, and the I8 listener with no pod-side caller until proposals 3 and 4 use them, which its tests cover through a test client. Proposal 3 depends on proposals 1, 2, and 9. Proposals 4, 5, and 6 depend on proposals 2 and 9, and proposals 4 and 5 also depend on proposal 3. Proposal 8 depends on proposals 4, 6, and 10. Proposal 12 depends on proposals 2 and 4. The shared gateway key-source proposal (F-7.1.31, the per-process `uploadToken` HMAC ring) is unaffected and can run first.

Proposal 7 depends on proposals 2 and 3, and on proposal 5 only for the mirror's service identity. Later work: push forwarding through the mirror, and GCP and Azure `metadata` plugins. R14 is a separate remediation step that this design does not absorb, and its serving certificate closes the service-mode residual when it lands. Revision 3 moves the DNS resolver for `proxied` pods and the server-name check into proposal 2, and TLS termination for bound hosts into proposal 4.

**Requests from other designs on the proposals above.** EG-1 carries the pre-mint step and the flavour refusal. EG-2 carries `cacheUntil`, the asserted route and its level 2 contract row, with its level 1 decision call in EG-7, the readable entries, `pkg/binding`'s user fields, the provisional exclusion, the link provider endpoints, and the head read deadline. EG-3 carries the push option refusal, client-visible remotes, and the exception for a session under `allowRuntimeHeldCredentials`. EG-4 carries `handling`, the asserted route and its I8 fields, the inspection stage, the typed `secret`, the one-binding rule, and the granted-entries rule on a binding mint. EG-5 carries the repository in I3. EG-9 carries the `userCredentialDelivery` gate, the delegated child exception, and `lockEgressToHosts`. EG-12 carries a `required` VCS pool as a pin source.

**Labels and dependencies across designs.** These proposals are EG-0 to EG-12 in other design documents, and each document states its own dependencies, which govern where this table is shorter. Proposals from different design documents can interleave, as long as these hold:

| Proposal | Depends on | Why |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| SO-1 (`security-auditor` role) | EG-8, EG-9 | The named permissions on the new reads |
| SO-2 (tenant guardrails) | EG-9, EG-2, EG-4, SO-1 | One write path for opt-ins, the suspended state, and the role that writes the record |
| SO-3 (per-tenant SIEM) | EG-8, EG-10 | The evidence export |
| FE-1 (quarantine) | EG-2 | The `quarantined` binding state |
| FE-2 (mirror store hold) | EG-5 | The held flag |
| FE-3 (platform-only holds) | EG-10 | The held marker on manifests |
| FE-4 (reconciler) | EG-2, EG-3, EG-5, EG-10 | The streams it reconciles |
| FE-6 (agent activity) | EG-10 | The envelope, ingest, and key field |
| TS-1 (environment scoping) | EG-1, EG-2, EG-3, EG-4, EG-5, EG-6, EG-9, and EG-8 for read filters | The objects it scopes and the resolver |
| OC-1 (connector) | EG-2, EG-6, EG-8, EG-9, EG-10, EG-11 | Typed route targets, the mint path, the forwarded route template, evidence, and certificates |
| OC-2 (registration) | EG-6, EG-8, EG-9, OC-1 | The endpoint record, the visibility reads, the policy objects, and the gateway |
| UG-1 (linking) | UC-1, EG-1, EG-2, EG-9, TS-6, and OC-1 and OC-2 for a private host | The digest format, VCS pool hosts, the mint path, the write path, and the login path |
| UG-2 (authorization) | UG-1, UC-3, EG-1, EG-2, EG-3, EG-5, TS-4 | The session input check, `retireOnRelease`, the mint path, `pkg/binding`, entries, the mirror, and the lifecycle hook |
| UG-3 (PR authoring) | UG-2, UC-2, UC-3, UC-4 | The binding and its rules |
| UG-4 (approval check) | UG-1, EG-3, EG-10 | `git.operation` |
| UG-5 (GitLab user authorship) | UG-3 | The template and spikes |
| UC-1 (types and `registered`) | EG-9 | The write path |
| UC-2 (`oauth2Link`) | UC-1, UG-1, EG-2, and OC-1 and OC-2 for a private host | The link flow and the mint path |
| UC-3 (`userCredential` binding) | UC-1, EG-2, EG-4, TS-4 | Bindings, I8, `pkg/binding`, and the invalidation level |
| UC-4 (request rules) | UC-3, EG-4 | The stage |
| UC-5 (`file`) | UC-3, EG-4, EG-9 | The mechanics and the gate |
| UC-6 (leak scanning) | UC-5 | Optional |

No proposal in this design depends on a proposal in another design.

## Documentation

The documentation has no owner page for egress or for VCS credentials today. Egress profiles live in a section of the operator guide's security page and are restated, with drift, on several other pages. VCS credentials appear as one paragraph in the concepts page and one row of the workspace plan reference. Lenny's documentation rules say that each concept has one owner page and every other page links to it (`doc-content.md`), so this subsystem gets a dedicated chapter, and the existing pages shrink to summaries that link into it. The pages describe behaviour for readers who have not read the specification, with no section numbers, so the proposals' spec changes land before the pages that document them. Each proposal in "Proposal split" carries the documentation changes for its own surface. The chapter's landing page and the egress policy page land with proposal 2.

### New chapter in the operator guide

A chapter titled "Network egress and credentials" sits in the operator guide after "Security". Its landing page states the reading order. The pages below are listed with the subject each owns. The detail they carry comes from this design and the landed specification, and is not repeated here.

| Page | Owns | Notes |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| Overview (landing) | The components and the path of a request from a tool in the pod to the internet, to a git host, and through an upstream proxy. Also the security properties in reader terms, and the reading order. | Diagrams, under `doc-diagram-style.md`, of the request path, the decision flow, and the git path. |
| Egress policy | Egress profiles, ceilings, host sets, warm-up policies, tenant egress policies, session host lists, and how the effective policy combines them. | Uses a subset of the scenarios as worked examples. States the exfiltration limit of hostname filtering. Becomes the owner of the content now in the security page's "Network Policies" section. |
| Evidence and visibility | The evidence streams, reading and exporting them, the effective-policy read, the shadow policy, and how to debug a refusal. | States what `self_reported` means, and that an abuse investigation on a shared address needs cloud NAT logs. |
| Tenant configuration | Who manages each setting, tenant pool overlays and their flags, tenant-registered public services, and platform-registered private services. | The reader-facing form of the scope table. |
| Git access | VCS credential pools, provider capability levels, repository prefixes, write scopes, stored-token push and its risks, which hosts are covered and what an uncovered host loses, SSH remotes, `gitAccess`, LFS, and the git mirror (enabling, sizing, prefetch, the fetch retry window, its Pod Security prerequisite, its anti-affinity and node count, what `hostUsers` does and does not protect against with its node requirements, and purge). | Includes a task section for setting up a GitHub App. Replaces the concepts page's description of gateway clones and the in-pod credential helper. |
| Credential bindings | The delivery modes, how to choose between them, placeholders, response scrubbing and its limits, and cloud credentials. | Distinguishes credential bindings from connector OAuth and from LLM credential pools, and links both. |
| Upstream proxies and corporate gateways | Chaining, egress groups and their grants, TLS interception and CA distribution, and replacing the default proxy with one that speaks the decision protocol. | States which identity methods each documented gateway accepts, as recorded in this design. |
| Level 1 proxies | The proxy contract, the policy document and the EDL output, identities and credential rotation, the probe, and the settings that select level 1. | Opens by stating that level 1 is not the recommended path. It then gives the table of what level 1 gives up against the default and level 2, the reasons, and when level 1 fits, and it directs most deployers to level 2 or chaining. Every page that describes a guarantee level 1 weakens links here. Runtimes need no change at level 1, and the runtime author guide says so. |
| Deploying and operating | CNI requirements, including AWS VPC CNI strict mode and the kindnet limits. Also the egress PKI (issuance, supplying Secrets without cert-manager, rotation, the readiness probe, and the expiry alert) and the `netagent` CPU limit rule. Also Envoy sizing (circuit breakers, timeouts, and the tunnel lifetime against LLM stream length), version pinning and advisories, metrics and alerts, and troubleshooting. | Troubleshooting covers a 403 traced through the decision log, a failed in-pod clone, and a tool that ignores the proxy. Links to the runbooks. |

### Runtime author guide

The runtime author guide gets one new page, "Network and credentials in the pod", which owns the runtime's view of the subsystem:

- the launch variables and the fixed endpoints;
- the `/run/netagent` layout and the network descriptor;
- the tools that need extra configuration, and baking tool configuration into an image;
- keeping the proxy and trust variables for spawned commands;
- git from inside the pod, including the bundle in git's trust, git in the image for `gh` even with `-R`, the libgit2 proxy option and hang on a constraint mismatch, JGit's lack of constraint enforcement, and the SSH failure on an uncovered host;
- credential bindings as the runtime sees them;
- the SDK accessor;
- the embedded model;
- behaviour under local development.

Existing pages change as follows:

| Page | Change |
| :-- | :-- |
| `index.md` | The reference runtime table loses the category-keyed framing ("same workspace layout, same pre-installed toolchains, same sandbox profile"). The `coding` scaffold template description drops platform defaults. The pod diagram gains `netagent`. Links to the new page. |
| `runtime-configuration.md` | `defaultPoolConfig` lists `proxied` and drops `provider-direct`. The `env` field states which variable names are reserved on `proxied` pools. |
| `lifecycle.md` | **Runtime Supervisor → environment** adds the launch variables. **Filesystem Layout** adds `/run/netagent`. **Workspace Materialization** describes the in-pod clone instead of gateway streaming for `gitClone` sources. The SDK-warm path notes that warm-up egress applies before claim. |
| `local-development.md` | States how `lenny up`, `make run`, and the compose stack run `netagent` and the egress proxy, and which enforcement is absent locally. |
| `integration-levels.md` | States that the network and credential mechanisms work at every integration level and need no level-specific code. |
| `runtime-sdk.md` and `sdk-examples/` | Document the network accessor, with one example per language. |
| `publishing.md` | The pool configuration table gains the egress fields, or links to the egress policy page. |
| `testing.md` | Describes the new conformance suite for network and credentials. |
| `platform-tools.md` | No change. `lenny/vcs_token` was never documented. |

The egress profile line, "No ambient network", and "no git-based workspace population" are updated. **Credentials** adds credential bindings and VCS credential pools as categories. **Platform-global vs tenant-scoped** mentions tenant pool overlays. |

Inbound anchors (`#network-policies`, `#dedicated-coredns`, and `#credential-leasing`) are kept or redirected. |

States the `egressProfile` write restriction. Egress groups are Helm values, documented in the operator guide and the Helm reference, and their assignments and the evidence reads are API resources here. |
